Borrows a Duchess","volume":"婚后外传：公爵夫人的失败伪装","anchor":"chapter-0250","paragraphs":["Mara borrowed Cassian's cap to avoid being recognized at the regatta. He borrowed her sunglasses for the same purpose. Neither disguise survived the first five minutes. Two former members of the national youth team recognized him while carrying a boat, then asked Mara whether she could help them get a camera case off a roof.","Lina and Jo were Asian-American women, now twenty-four and twenty-five, who had come to coach the local crews. Their visiting friends included Hispanics from a coastal sports club. The terms were the women's own descriptions, offered to a reporter who had asked how they wished to be identified. They were far more interested in the race than the labels. Mara agreed to take care of the case while they trained.","A private company sold media packages to event sponsors. Its manager had cut together a clip of Jo stumbling on a wet landing, framing her as lazy. The video was brilliant bait for attention and pernicious advertising for the club. Its view count had grown eightfold. Frequent reposts could amplify a false impression far beyond the original audience. The halo effect of a handsome logo did not make the editing accurate.","The clip had abundant views and no context. Its original did not coincide with the manager's account. A back-up copy might help, provided someone could find it. June's subscription service stored raw recordings after upload. The assistant was aware of its existence; he had not been allowed to use the company's connection. Another obstacle could accrue to his growing list, Mara thought, or somebody could lend him a phone.","The manager casually called the clip harmless. Mara asked to see the full footage. He refused. He was going to keep the camera case where it was until the athletes agreed to an interview. The implied bargain led her patience astray. “I aim to retrieve their property,” she said. “Anything less than returning it is not an answer.”","His claim was all very well as a distraction. It did not attain the simple result the women needed. Lina, an advanced rower, was not willing to become a prop. Her brilliant question was whether he would return the case now or let the caretaker do it. The manager looked alternately at her and Cassian, trying to calculate which one would be easier to intimidate.","Cassian was already looking for the roof stairs. Jo beat him to the punch by finding a back-up key with the caretaker. Her spot-on knowledge of the building made the manager's grand performance ineffectual. Mara smiled, and the man visibly wished he had chosen a different morning.","The little-known roof route was shown on an old dimensional drawing. The caretaker added a pencil mark to the map. He could devise an option that did not lead anyone astray through a locked workshop. Mara thought the idea spot-on. A tolerant colleague could help without accepting ever-tougher demands from a man who had no authority to make them.","Ironically, the manager could not relate his own clip to its source without changing his account dramatically. It might be similar to a joke, but millions of viewers would not know the context. June would integrate the original sequence. She certainly would not get into trouble with her editor for asking the right question. A string of excuses was not a source.","The festival occurred annually beside a man-made harbour. Its fishing boats depended on healthy fish stocks and predictable access. A far-off trawler passed the mouth while children practised starts in the shallows. The concurrent traffic required careful coordination. The harbourmaster would not deregulate the route merely because a sponsor found boundaries inconvenient. Inside, the missing camera case lay beside several borrowed phones and a cash box. The caretaker called the police. Mara stayed by the door with Jo, while Cassian helped the frightened camera assistant identify his things. A pledge to return everything would not erase the discovery.","The cupboard contained abundant things that did not originate from the company. Jo began to relate each item to its owner. An advanced camera had a name scratched underneath; the phones were easier. The return was not going to attain perfection instantly. Their back-up list would let them check the little-known cases without inventing who owned them.","The company's manager tried to leave by the pontoon. Lina, one of the two Asian-American women who had returned from the national youth team, saw him first. Her friends, including several Hispanics from the visiting club, were already moving boats. They cleared a path for the guard instead of surrounding the man. Their priority was to keep everyone out of the water.","His attempt to jump aboard a launch failed dramatically. The fender rolled beneath his shoe. He landed in the shallows with a roar, then discovered he could stand. Mara would take care of his safety despite finding his behaviour intolerable. Cassian offered a hand. The look exchanged between the men would not improve the manager's temper.","Mara thought anything less than getting him safely ashore would be shameful. His disruptive choices did not remove his right to help. She would not be biased against a man merely because she disliked him. Her tolerant response did not mean she accepted his claims. Ironically, his safest way out was cooperation with the people he had insulted.","The police identified the new arrestee and took statements. Whether to prosecute would be decided through the proper process. The manager's former threats had no useful role now. The city inquiry by the Justice Department remained unrelated; June refused to let a shared surname turn two cases into one headline. Her care was spot-on and unglamorous.","The private company sold media space in the event programme. It would not be allowed to keep the footage merely because a payment was pending. Its ex-coach asked staff to cooperate with the equipment return. Labour-management relations might worsen before they improved, but nobody had to surrender their personal phone to finish a shift. The camera assistant would receive his paycheck on the promised date.","The company would not deduct the fee before discussing the missing property. The assistant wanted to get into trouble with nobody, least of all his boss. An ex-teacher among the visitors helped him preserve his own notes. She knew how a gorgeous leaflet could motivate someone to accept ever-tougher terms. The words were all very well. Their effect on actual work needed checking.","The original film showed Jo dodging a trolley before slipping. Its broader context would add a crucial difference to the story. The manager's edit had been biased against her from the outset. The halo effect of professional design had concealed an unrepresentative selection. Millions of viewers could still have seen an incomplete story if nobody checked the source.","A dimensional drawing showed the gap Jo had stepped across. It was narrow, wet and obstructed. The manager could not integrate that fact into his original story because it would undo the joke. June was not going to help him preserve it. A pearl of advice from her first editor returned: look at the sequence before writing the character. The rule was simple enough to keep.","The event happened annually, and the problems would accrue if nobody noticed. The caretaker was aware of that. He looked alternately at the old latch and the new damage. A lost key could lead a visitor astray; this required something more deliberate. In some cases, a practical repair was enough. Here an ineffectual apology would not ensure the missing phones returned.","A child shouted “Go forth!” at a toy boat. The manager's abandoned leaflet fluttered beside it, its claim to be a best-seller suddenly absurd. The camera assistant would rather apologise than argue about a tax receipt. Mara offered a bit of patience. He was allowed to finish one task before accepting another.","June aimed to publish a correction. Frequent online repetition could amplify a pernicious claim; another dramatic accusation would not necessarily repair it. The view count had risen eightfold, and the response might be similar to another burst of publicity. Her editor asked her to integrate the full sequence and let readers see the difference. She would critically examine the claim instead of polishing its appearance.","Jo did not want to become an idealized victim either. She was an advanced rower, occasionally impatient and perfectly capable of being lazy on a free morning. None of that made the clip fair. The distinction seemed to elude a spectator who demanded a flawless heroine. Mara asked him to civilize his manners before assessing hers.","The harbour's man-made gate was closing as the cyclic tide changed. Its movement had to coincide with a temporary pause in racing. Concurrent fishing traffic would use the other route. A proposal to deregulate access had been discussed last year, but the harbourmaster had rejected it to protect fish stocks and crews alike. Mara appreciated a rule that constrained the strongest boat too.","The far-off boat changed course as a marshal raised a flag. The concurrent movement would not constrain the rowers once it had passed. A child wanted to know whether the flags were similar to traffic lights. They were, in a broad sense, though the exact signals differed. Cassian explained only what he knew and left the rest to the marshal.","The athletes reorganized their warm-up. “We are going to race,” Jo said. “Not give him the rest of the day.”","The camera assistant casually called Jo's restraint brilliant. She shrugged. She had no need to compute the value of every insult. Keeping her temper in check helped more. The guard ought to receive clear information, not another performance. The result might be inconclusive for now, but uncertainty was not a reason to add a rumour to the account.","Mara agreed. She expected them to enjoy themselves, not win to justify their anger. The little-known volunteer who suggested it received more gratitude than any sponsor's speech.","The two Asian-American women celebrated with their visiting friends. Several Hispanics from the club wanted a group photograph with the local rowers. June asked everyone individually about permission. The resulting picture was less arranged than the sponsor's idealized version and far more alive. Mara liked the athlete grinning through a mouthful of cake best.","The club aimed to keep the event annually, with an equipment agreement that could ensure people retained control of their own work. Jo thought that an intelligent use of an awkward day.","Benefits would accrue slowly if the new agreement worked. Jo was aware of that. It was all very well to announce a brilliant reform; someone had to integrate it into the next shift. The club would review the programme annually. Its frequent visitors would notice if nothing changed. They were a better guide than one unrepresentative group selected for a photograph.","The arrestee had been taken away. The club would provide accounts and cooperate with the investigation. It would not decide whether to prosecute from a race announcement. June kept the unrelated city story separate. Her editor had tried to relate the two through a shared location. She explained why that was not enough, then returned to photographing the boats.","The edited clip's reach had grown eightfold before it was removed from the company's page. Other copies might remain. Its pernicious effect could not be computed from a single count. A correction needed to amplify the right context, but it could not ensure every viewer saw it. The club would not let that uncertainty make its response ineffectual.","June looked critically at the final sequence. She did not want its correction to go astray through another dramatic simplification. The facts did not originate from one perfect witness. They came from several people, a back-up file and the original film. She was going to make the distinction clear, even if a more idealized ending would attract clicks.","June would not become biased against every sponsor. Some offered ever-tougher terms; others helped quietly. In some cases, motives remained inconclusive. A tolerant reporter could admit that without becoming timid. She kept her annoyance in check and asked how the club would reorganize its media work. Its former agreement had begun to disintegrate, but the people could still unify around a better one.","Cassian looked dramatically wounded when she borrowed his drink. She ought to apologise, he said. Her smile suggested a different plan. The little joke did not derive from any dispute she could recall. They simply enjoyed finding each other at the end of a long day. Specifically, he enjoyed it more when nobody asked him for another photograph.","The race results were abundant evidence of ordinary variation. The cyclic change of water still governed its route. Fish stocks depended on more than today's quiet surface, and a local survey would continue through winter. The harbourmaster said they would not deregulate the area to match a sponsor's impatience. Mara liked the consistency of that answer.","The tide's high point would coincide with the concurrent return of two crews. A marshal's spot-on timing kept the entrance clear. The child wanted the names of two people who had helped her tie a boat. The halo effect of a good afternoon seemed kinder when nobody tried to turn it into a universal truth.","Cassian found Mara beside the last empty bench. His hand rested at her waist. She turned to him and kissed him before he could propose another task. He had already taken care of enough things. Crocodile","anchor":"chapter-0253","paragraphs":["Idris Kade, a visiting adult engineer from Larkhaven, went to the flower parade to repair a pump. By ten, he was wearing a crown of daisies and arguing with a beautiful woman about whether a boat could have hips. \"It can have a broad beam,\" he said. \"Hips would be intellectualism without engineering.\" Paint covered old decay, and erosion had altered the landing nearby.","\"My boat has excellent hips,\" Rhea Dane answered. \"Your vocabulary is irrelevant.\" Her grin seemed unstoppable.","Her aging skiff lay beside the canal, blossoming under paper lilies. The annual contest celebrated the river's role in civilisation. One float depicted globalisation as a dragon swallowing shipping containers; another tackled global warming with a polar bear demanding shade. The synthesis was odd but cheerful. Rhea's plan was simpler: take a toothless crocodile through the course and win enough money to keep her workshop open.","The crocodile had a budding apple tree painted along its back. Rhea called the growth a symbol of stubborn business. A reproduction of a photograph from the late 1970s showed her father beside the same hull. Its evolution had been unconventional; in contrast, its repairs followed ordinary rules. In terms of beauty, she would challenge any swan. Idris had to admire that confidence.","The private sector had lately offered her more speeches than work. The shipyard expansion after a deregulatory reform had ended in a slump, and she had got laid off with most of its repair crew. Her former managerial duties had included maintaining boats larger than this one. Idris would not flatter her by calling her helpless. He asked what she needed.","\"A pump that doesn't frizzle its wiring,\" she said. \"And someone to halve my mother's enthusiasm.\"","Her mother, Devi, appeared carrying spicy buns. She had spent thirty years assisting childbirth and claimed that panicked boatbuilders were every bit as noisy as expectant fathers. She was native-born and had lived in the river district long before it became fashionable. Historically, she said, the canal had entertained a bit of everything, including worse crocodiles. The harmless boast made her daughter smile.","Idris's apprehension diminished when he saw the shallow water and checked boarding arrangements. He preferred a solid workshop floor to an unfamiliar skiff, but coordinated work helped him more than bravado. He would not mindlessly declare himself cured. Rhea watched him assess the route without teasing. The consideration made him oddly conscious of his own hands. The identifiable scrape on the hose was evidence of cyclical wear.","The pump's extraction hose had clogged with paper. Its inefficient filter had allowed an accumulation of decorative scraps. Idris removed the debris and ran a reproducible test in a bucket. Rhea took the feedback seriously. Her orthodoxy was practical: if a pretty design prevented access to a vital part, change the design. He respected that as much as he liked her grin.","A laminated notice warned, BEWARE OF THE SWAN. The real bird had claimed a distant stretch of bank and would not be approached. The course avoided it. A governmental permit fixed the turns, despite the parade sponsor's desire for a unilateral shortcut. The sponsor's own inconsistent instructions had confused two crews. Idris asked the coordinator to clarify them before launch.","He would not claim to know the whole boat from one inspection. Its drain could lead to a leak if ignored, and the pump must eject water freely. The waste bin said DUMP NOTHING IN THE RIVER. Deliberate dumping had been prosecuted, a notice added. Rhea tried to live by the same rule even when paper petals escaped. She would not perpetuate somebody else's mess.","Rhea's former boss arrived in a blaze of white linen. He wanted to criticize her float as too modest. His own giant corporate swan required twelve people and a noisy fan. It would expedite the parade, he claimed, if smaller craft withdrew. Devi offered him a bun, hot enough to make the flame of his complaint dissipate into coughing.","\"Don't exaggerate,\" Rhea told her mother. \"He won't melt.\"","\"A reaction worth observing.\" Fragrant buns made the challenging task of waiting easier.","The man called the performance solely comic. Rhea conceded that a crocodile in a bonnet was not orthodox grandeur. Yet laughter was a legitimate form of public entertainment. It did not diminish her skill. Idris unexpectedly agreed aloud. The old boss looked from him to her, then walked away.","Rhea's cheek color deepened. \"You're unusually good at not being impressed.\"","\"Only by people who try to impress me.\"","The boat shifted when a child leaned over its side. Idris steadied it and asked the girl to step back. A stagnant pocket of water lay inside the hollow tail, where the drain had been blocked by a ribbon. Rhea's vivid paint concealed the opening. The interference was coincident with the pump trouble, not its cause, but both arose from the same decorative enthusiasm. Devi's concurring opinion was that the tail should lose the bow.","The small problem could have a far-reaching effect if ignored. Idris did not deem it trivial, and Rhea did not resent the correction. Their seemingly different methods began to relate to each other. She made room for his tools; he preserved the bonnet while clearing the drain. Relaxation took hold as the work became a shared rhythm.","The speaker announced the start. Inflation had drastically increased the entry cost, Devi complained, while the prize had stayed stagnant since the late 1970s. Her numbers were probably theatrical, but her enthusiasm was contagious. She handed Idris a final bun and told him to soak up the applause. He almost smiled before remembering the boat still had to pass its inspection.","Rhea's retrieval line snapped free. A paper lily drifted across the water, precious to nobody except the child who had made it. Idris reached with a pole and brought it back. The girl's relief was intelligible without words. Rhea watched him with a warmth he could not blame on the sun.","\"We ought to board,\" she said.","He glanced at the water, then at her outstretched hand. \"We can go further than the bank.\"","\"A stirring defence of my boat.\"","He stepped aboard. Behind them, Devi's laugh rolled over the canal like a blessing."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1037,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0253\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div
